L-BFGS via scipy with custom function:
def fun_and_grad(x):
    u = x - x.max()
    a = exp(u); a /= sum
    b = conv(a)
    max_b = b.max()
    # LSE
    tau = current_tau (nonlocal)
    e = exp(tau*(b-max_b))
    sum_e = e.sum()
    val = max_b + log(sum_e)/tau
    # weights
    if active: p = e/sum_e else ifft...
    g = 4 * ...
    grad = a * (g - np.dot(a,g))  # since sum a=1
    return val, grad

Potential algorithm:
1. Generate candidate supports:
   - Random subset of size m in [0,n-1] for various n/m (m maybe 20-200); or greedy Sidon-like: start with 0, add point that minimizes max additional pair sum multiplicity (or total slack) for a grid/continuous n. We can construct a support with low additive energy.
   - For each support, solve weight optimization to minimize max b with sum=1.
2. Weight optimization on support:
   Variables w∈R^m. Use ξ parameterization xi = log w + maybe? Objective f=max_k b_k(w) (scale invariant). We can use custom L-BFGS or projected gradient with simplex constraint (w sum=1). Since m small, direct convolution of support positions: b_k = ∑_{i,j: pos_i+pos_j=k} w_i w_j. We can precompute for each pair (i,j) the bin k (pos_i+pos_j) and coefficient: +1 if i==j else +2? Because polynomial square coefficient (ordered) includes w_i w_j and w_j w_i. For i<j, contribution 2 w_i w_j. We can precompute lists: for each bin k, list of pairs indices (i,j) with i≤j, weight factor c (1 if i=j else 2). Then b = C(w). Since m maybe 200, number of pairs ~20k. We can compute b in O(num_pairs) if update, but for gradient each step O(num_pairs*m?)? Actually b_k quadratic, gradient wrt w: for each pair contribution c w_i w_j at bin k, gradient: if i=j: 2 c? For c=1 diagonal contribution w_i², grad_i=2w_i. For offdiag c=2: contribution 2 w_i w_j, grad_i=2w_j, grad_j=2w_i. For max/smoothed weights p_k, gradient = ∑ p_k * per-pair gradients. Can accumulate by iterating pairs O(m²). Fine for m~200 ->40k per gradient; 100k evals =4e9 too high but okay maybe in Python? Need vectorize.

Can use matrix representation: Let positions sorted. b_k = w^T B_k w with B_k sparse. But direct pair loop in Python might be slow. Better use dense convolution: w_full length n with zeros at support; F_t? If n up to maybe 5000, use FFT: a = zeros(n), a[support]=w, b = convolve(a,a) via FFT O(n log n); gradient g via conv of p and a; then projected gradient on support variables. This is simpler and efficient for m稀疏, n maybe 1000. Precompute positions; after each update set a on support. If n small, direct np.convolve also fine, but FFT.

Represent support positions p (int array length m). Precompute coefficient of b for each pair i,j: k = p_i + p_j, factor = 1 if i==j else 2. Then for normalized weights w (sum1), b_k = Σ factor w_i w_j for pairs with sum k. For gradient wrt w for max/smoothed: For LSE weights P over k, derivative of b_k: grad[k] for variables? We can precompute pair lists by sum: for each k, pairs (i,j,factor). Then gradient g_i = Σ_{k} P_k * (Σ_{j: p_i+p_j=k} factor' * w_j?) More directly: b = w^T B w? For each sum k, B_k has entries 1/2 factor? Let's use pair contribution:
For i<j: contribution c=2 w_i w_j to bin k=p_i+p_j. Gradient wrt w_i +=2 P_k w_j, wrt w_j +=2 P_k w_i. For i=i: contribution w_i²; gradient +=2 P_k w_i.
So g_i = 2 Σ_{k} P_k Σ_{j: k=p_i+p_j} (if j==i then w_j else w_j?) For all j with p_i+p_j=k, grad_i +=2 P_k w_j. That's neat. If we have P across bins, for each pair (i,j,k) with factor? gradient_i +=2 P_k w_j, gradient_j +=2 P_k w_i, but for i=j double? For diagonal contribution w_i², gradient_i=2 w_i, one pair. If we iterate all i,j (ordered?) b includes ordered pairs? Let's define b_k = Σ_{i,j: p_i+p_j=k} w_i w_j (ordered). Then gradient_i=2 Σ_{j:p_i+p_j=k} P_k w_j. Iterate all ordered pairs m²: for each i,j add to g_i 2P_k w_j. This is O(m²). For m=200, 40k per eval. In Python loops too slow for many.    # Use direct active if eff small else FFT
   g = 4*crosscorr(P,a)

   update x with Adam. Optionally fix x max? Use no.
   track exact score (2n*max_b) when sum w=1; update best.
- At end, convert a full and evaluate exact.

Let's implement a generic search and hope. We can also include fallback to return parent if nothing better. To maximize chance, combine:

Let's design component implementations carefully.

Convolutions:
- For n up to maybe 8192, use FFT with length L = 1 << (2n-1).bit_length(). For n=5582 L=16384.
- `linear_convolve(a)`:
   A = np.fft.rfft(a, L); b = np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:2*n-1]; return b.
Maybe np.fft has wrap errors; clip small negative? b nonnegative but roundoff may tiny negative. Objective uses max; negative irrelevant. For gradient, ensure nonnegative not required.
- For exact evaluation use `evaluate_sequence` to avoid mismatch; it uses np.convolve. Our internal b may differ due to FFT rounding; track best by exact evaluator periodically.

Smooth objective on a sum1:
```
def smooth_data(a, tau, use_direct=True, topk=200):
    n=a.size; b=conv(a); m=b.max()
    e=np.exp(tau*(b-m)); s=e.sum(); p=e/s
    return m, p
```
Weights p sum1. Gradient:
```
def grad_from_p(p,a,n,L):
    if effective support len < direct_threshold:
       g=np.zeros(n)
       idx=np.nonzero(p>eps)[0]

          pk=p[k]
          if k < n:
             seg=a[k::-1]  # a[0..k]
          else:
             seg=a[n-1:k-n::-? need]

Adam implementation details:
```
def adam_step(x, grad, m, v, t, beta1, beta2, eps, lr): ...
```
We need x array length m/dim. For full n, x length n. Use lr maybe 0.05? Variables x on log-softmax. Effective step in a proportional to lr*a*grad. To reduce max b, scale: grad ~ O(4 * maybe w)?? For w~1/n, b~1/n, p active, g ~4 a ~4/n ≈ 7e-4. grad_x ~ w*(grad) ~1/n *7e-4=1.3e-7. Adam normalizes scale, step in x ~lr. Need lr maybe 0.001-0.05. However x shifts cause a changes proportional to lr. We can tune.

But objective scale invariant; x large values can cause softmax saturation. We can periodically re-center x by subtracting max/mean to avoid overflow. Since softmax invariant, x -= x.mean() maybe.

Track best a: exact score via evaluator costly but can use internal score = 2n*max_b (since sum1) and later exact. To update best, if internal_score < best_internal*(1+tol) and exact? We can also just use internal max for best, final verify. But due to FFT rounding, internal may be slightly low; exact evaluator might be higher by epsilon. Use best internal and final exact; if exact worse, still. Could keep parent fallback.

We can also use a deterministic exact local random search from best: random multiplicative perturbations of blocks with exact evaluate, similar previous, but now on best sparse. This may improve.

Need choose number of optimization iterations/time. Budget 1300s; leave 30s for final validation. Use time checks.

Let's design function `optimize_full_sequence(n, x_init, steps, tau, lr, ...)`:
- Precompute L for FFT length 2n-1 for convolve b. L2 for gradient if needed maybe 32768; but direct usually.
- a = softmax(x); best_a=a; best_score_internal=2n*max_b.
- For it in range(steps):
   internal time check if time > deadline break.
   compute b, max_b, p.
   if max_b improved: maybe update.
   compute p active: use softmax weights with tau_eff = tau; but if exp all underflow? subtract max.
   p = e/s; if p.max too high okay.
   if effective support < threshold (e.g., len with p>1e-6 < 5000) direct else FFT.
   g = ...
   grad_x = a*(g - np.dot(a,g))
   Adam update x.
   recenter x -= np.mean(x)
- At end return best_a.

But if p active only single max and gradient constant? Example symmetric sequences may have p at bin k, g segment may not constant; Adam can move. For uniform, p central, g constant, grad_x zero; stuck. Need avoid starting exactly uniform. Random x with std enough.

Initializations for full n:
- From parent: use normalized clipped. But parent may be near uniform? If score2, maybe uniform. We can perturb it.
- Generate random Dirichlet-like x:
   For many configs: base = random normal size n with std σ; plus maybe block/spatial structure (low-pass via FFT random phases?) To create nonuniform patterns, use fGbm? Simpler: sort random? We need high effective support but nonuniform. Use x = random.normal(0, σ) + offset maybe; then add a low-frequency component: x += A*cos(2π f i/n + phase) to break central symmetry. Since nonnegative after softmax, adding cosine can create alternating nonuniform.
   Also sparse starts: choose m random active indices set x=0 there, -large elsewhere. m maybe n/10, n/5, sqrt(n), etc.
- Run optimizer for a few hundred steps each.

Helper functions:
```
def _softmax(x):
    x = x - x.max()
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: return np.full_like(x,1.0/x.size)
    return e/s
```
For large x, recenter before.

```
def _make_conv(n):
    L = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    def conv(a):
        A=np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
        b=np.fft.irfft(A*A, n=L)[:2*n-1]
        # remove tiny negatives
        # b = np.where(b>0,b,0)? For max positive, leave.
        return b
    return conv
```
But creating function closures per n; many configs. Could just compute L each call; L bit length cheap.

For support optimization, a is full length n dense with zeros; constructing each call O(n). We can represent a and update support:
```
a=np.zeros(n); a[pos]=_softmax(x)
b=conv(a)
```
Consecutive calls set entire a; okay.

Gradient direct active:
```
def _gradient_from_p(p,a,n,direct_threshold=5000, Lg=None):
    # p length 2n-1
    # Use direct if number of active p small.
    # Active by threshold p > max(p)*1e-8? and cumsum?
    if np.sum(p*p) > 1e-12:
        eff = 1.0/np.sum(p*p)
    else: eff = len(p)
    if eff < direct_threshold or n <= 300:
       idx = np.where(p > max(1e-12, p.max()*1e-8))[0]
       # Maybe limit to top max(20, int(eff*1.5))
       if len(idx)>max(20, direct_threshold//2):
           # sort by p desc
           order=np.argsort(p)[::-1][:direct_threshold]
           idx=order
       pfallout=1.0/len(p)
       g=np.zeros(n)
       for k in idx:
          pk=p[k]
          if pk <=0: continue
          pk4=4.0*pk
          if k < n:
             g[:k+1] += pk4 * a[k::-1]
          else:
             x0=k-n+1
             if x0 < n:
                g[x0:n] += pk4 * a[n-1:x0-1:-1]
       # Optional add uniform missing? Uniform gradient constant ignored. But if idx missing large total p? If p has dense mass not included, constant plus small variation? We include top by p; missing low p may not matter. To be more accurate, if missing mass >0.05 and n small, dense FFT.
       return g
    else:
       # FFT using length Lg next power of (3n-2).bit_length()
       m=2*n-1
       p_rev=p[::-1]
       Lg=1 << (3*n-2).bit_length()
       Fa=np.fft.rfft(a, n=Lg)
       Fp=np.fft.rfft(p_rev, n=Lg)
       c=np.fft.irfft(Fa*Fp, n=Lg)
       # v_j = c[m-1-j]
       g=4.0 * c[m-1:m-n-1:-1]  # length n? If m-1 down to m-n inclusive
       return g
```
Check slice: m=2n-1. m-1=2n-2. Need indices from 2n-2 down to (2n-2)-(n-1)=n-1. Slice `c[2*n-2 : n-2 : -1]`? If starting 2n-2, stopping n-2 exclusive, step -1 gives length n? Example n=5,m=9,c indices 8,7,6,5,4? Wait m-1=8, want 8,7,6,5,4? For n=5, j=0..4 correspond indices 8-j: 8,7,6,5,4. So slice `c[m-1 : m-n-1 : -1]` because stop=m-n-1=3 exclusive; gives 8,7,6,5,4. Formula m-n-1 =2n-1-n-1=n-2. Good. Use `c[m-1 : m-n-1 : -1]`. Ensure length n.

But convolution p_rev * a via FFT with length Lg: c linear length 3n-2. Good.

Potential issue: p not normalized to sum1? p from LSE normalized; direct sums p over idx maybe <1 if truncated. Since uniform component omitted constant? Missing low p not constant; but if truncated top threshold maybe missing mass low and variation small. Could renormalize p over included? If we renormalize active top to sum1, we bias toward active; uniform missing ignored. That's okay for approximate subgradient. Use p_act = p[idx]/p[idx].sum()? If missing mass, top overweights. But maybe okay. To be safer, if n small use all nonzero; if large and p effective small, missing mass tiny. We can include all p > p.max()*1e-6. Eff support threshold ensures total mass mostly.

LSE p generation:
```
def _lse_weights(b, max_b, tau, floor=1e-12):
    e=np.exp(tau*(b - max_b))
    s=e.sum()
    if not finite or s==0:
       # uniform over max bins
       mask=b>=max_b-1e-15
       p=np.zeros_like(b); p[mask]=1/mask.count
    else: p=e/s
    return p
```

def normalized_sequence_from_array(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<1e-300: return np.ones(1)
    return a/s

def try_update(a, label=''):
    arr = np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    if arr.size==0: return
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0, posinf=1000, neginf=0)
    arr = np.clip(arr,0,1000)
    # trim leading/trailing below tol
    if arr.size>1:
       tol = max(1e-15, arr.max()*1e-12)
       first = np.argmax(arr > tol) if np.any(arr>tol) else 0
       # find last: reversed
       left = arr>tol
       if left.any():
           first = int(np.argmax(left)); last = int(arr.size - 1 - np.argmax(left[::-1]))
           arr = arr[first:last+1]
       else: arr=np.array([1.0])
    s=arr.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
       arr=np.ones(max(1, arr.size)); s=arr.size
    # do not normalize? For exact score scale invariant, but if arr sum huge? Clip maybe. We can normalize to sum1 to avoid large conv sum overflow? Evaluator clips but not normalize; if arr values 1000 and many, sum huge score still finite but convolution may overflow? To be safe, scale to sum=1 if s>0: arr=arr/s.
    arr=arr/s
    if not np.isfinite(arr).all() or arr.size==0: return
    score=evaluate_sequence(arr.tolist())
    if np.isfinite(score) and score < state['score']:
       state['score']=float(score); state['seq']=arr.copy()
```

def run_lbbfsgs(n,pos,x0,maxfun,deadline,fun_cost_estimate=0.005):
   m=pos.size
   conv=make_conv(n)
   a=np.zeros(n)
   # initial a
   x=np.asarray(x0).copy(); x -= x.mean()
   w=softmax(x); a[pos]=w; b=conv(a); mb0=b.max(); tau=max(5000, 30/mb0)
   best_val=np.inf; best_x=x.copy()
   call_count=0
   def fun(x):
      nonlocal call_count, best_val,best_x
      if time.monotonic() > deadline: return 1e100, np.zeros(len(x))  # terminate? scipy may raise? Better stop via callback raising custom? Return value won't stop. Use callback and options maxfun enough.
      x=np.asarray(x).copy()
      if not np.isfinite(x).all(): return 1e100, np.zeros(len(x))
      x -= x.mean()
      w=softmax(x)
      a[pos]=w
      b=conv(a); mb=b.max()
      e=np.exp(tau*(b-mb)); s=e.sum()
      if not np.isfinite(s) or s==0: p=np.zeros(2*n-1); p[np.isclose(b,mb)] = ...
      else: p=e/s
      val=mb + np.log(s)/tau
      if val < best_val: best_val=val; best_x=x.copy()
      call_count +=1
      if p effective... grad
      g = gradient...
      gp=g[pos]; gradx=w*(gp - np.dot(w,gp))
      if not np.isfinite(gradx).all(): return val, np.zeros(len(x))
      return val, gradx
   try:
      res=scipy.optimize.minimize(fun, x, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={'maxfun': maxfun, 'ftol':1e-9, 'gtol':1e-7, 'maxiter': maxfun, 'disp': False})
   except Exception: pass
   # evaluate fun(best_x) maybe
```

Let's code `run_adam(n, pos, x0, steps, lr0, deadline, check_exact_cb=None, rng, stats)`:
Detailed:
```
def run_adam(n,pos,x0,steps,lr0,deadline,rng,stats):
    m=pos.size
    x=np.asarray(x0,dtype=float).copy()
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): x=np.zeros(m)
    x -= np.mean(x)
    a=np.zeros(n) if m<n else None
    w=softmax(x); if m==n and pos is all? a=w else a[pos]=w
    b=conv(a); mb=b.max(); score=2*n*mb
    best_x=x.copy(); best_a=a.copy(); best_score=score; best_it=0; last_improve=0
    adam_m=np.zeros(m); adam_v=np.zeros(m); t=0
    beta1=0.9; beta2=0.99; eps=1e-8
    restarts=0; patience=max(100, steps//10)
    noise_scale=0.2
    it=0
    while it<steps and time<deadline:
       # compute p, grad from current b,mb
       p = lse_weights(b, mb)
       g = grad_from_p(p,a,n)
       gp = g[pos] if m<n else g
       wg = float(np.dot(w,gp))
       grad = w*(gp - wg)
       if not finite: break
       t +=1
       mhat=...; vhat=...
       lr=lr0*(0.9? decay)
       x -= lr*mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+eps)
       # if any nan break
       x[~finite]=0
       # recenter periodically (each step)
       x -= np.mean(x)
       w=softmax(x)
       if m==n:
           a=w
       else:
           a[pos]=w
       b=conv(a); mb=b.max(); score=2*n*mb
       if score < best_score*(1-1e-12) or not finite(best_score):
           best_score=score; best_x=x.copy(); best_a=a.copy(); last_improve=it
       it +=1
       if it - last_improve > patience:
           # periodically evaluate? maybe break if restarts>4
           if restarts>=5: break
           x=best_x.copy()
           # noise
           x += rng.normal(0, noise_scale, m)
           x -= x.mean()
           adam_m*=0; adam_v*=0; t=0
           restarts += 1
           patience = max(50, patience//2)
           noise_scale *=0.7
       # stats update cost
    return best_a, best_score
```

`lse_weights` with tau maybe based on mb. Since mb updated each step, tau changes. Good.
```
def lse_weights(b, mb):
   if mb <= 0: return np.full(b.size,1/b.size)
   tau = max(10000.0, 30.0 / mb)
   tau = min(tau, 1e7)
   e = np.exp(tau*(b - mb))
   s=e.sum()
   if s==0 or not isfinite: active=np.where(b==mb); p=zeros; p[active]=1/active.size
   else p=e/s
   return p
```

def gen_x(m, rng, mode):
   x = np.zeros(m)
   if mode == 0: # equal
       pass
   elif mode == 1: # random normal
       x = rng.normal(0,1.0,m)
   elif mode == 2: # smooth random over sorted support
       f=len(x)//2+1; spec=...; x=irfft; x-=mean
       # if m=0? no
   elif mode == 3: # sparse spike within support
       x = -6*np.ones(m)
       k=max(1,m//4); idx=rng.choice(m,k,replace=False); x[idx]=rng.normal(0,1)
   elif mode == 4: # logarithmic ramp + random
       x = np.linspace(2, -2, m) + rng.normal(0,0.5,m)
   x -= x.mean()
   return x
```

Let's code `trim`:
```
def trim_array(a, rel=1e-12):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size==0: return np.array([1.0])
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    if a.size>1:
       maxv=a.max()
       if maxv>0:
          tol=max(1e-15, maxv*rel)
          mask=a>tol
          if mask.any():
             first=int(np.argmax(mask))
             last=int(a.size-1-np.argmax(mask[::-1]))
             if first>0 or last<a.size-1:
                 a=a[first:last+1]
          else:
             idx=int(np.argmax(a)); a=a[idx:idx+1]
    s=a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0:
       return np.array([1.0])
    # normalize to sum1? It might reduce overflow. Do: a=a/s
    return a / s
```
This always normalizes to sum1; score invariant, except if clipping? All values <=1 maybe. Good. If max huge, normalize; evaluator sum large? Score same. If a very small after normalize? no.

def local_search(state, deadline):
   a=np.asarray(state['seq'],float); n=a.size
   best=a.copy(); best_score=state['score']

   max_iters = ...

      c=best.copy()
      move_type=rng.integers(0,5)
      if move_type==0: block scale
      elif 1: single scale
      elif 2: move mass from i to j
      elif 3: add small gaussian
      else: random permutation? Not.
      c=trim_array(c)
      score=evaluate_sequence(c.tolist())
      if score < best_score: best=c; best_score=score; update state
```

Implement `_gradient_from_p(p,a,n)`:
Need if n<2? direct.
```
def _gradient_from_p(p,a,n):
    g=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0: return g
    pmax=float(p.max())
    if pmax <= 0: return g
    thresh=max(1e-15, pmax*1e-7)
    active=np.nonzero(p>thresh)[0]
    # If active too large, FFT
    max_active = max(64, min(5000, 2000000 // max(1,n)))
    if active.size <= max_active:
       for k in active:
          pk=float(p[k])
          if pk <= 0: continue
          f=2.0*pk
          kk=int(k)
          if kk < n:
             g[:kk+1] += f * a[kk::-1]
          else:
             x0=kk - n + 1
             if x0 < n:
                g[x0:n] += f * a[n-1:x0-1:-1]
       return g
    # else FFT
    m=2*n-1
    L=1 << (3*n - 2).bit_length()
    p_rev=p[::-1]
    Fa=np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
    Fp=np.fft.rfft(p_rev, n=L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(Fa*Fp, n=L)
    g[:] = 2.0 * c[m-1:m-n-1:-1]
    return g
```
Check slice stop: m-n-1. For n=5, m=9, m-n-1=3, c[8:3:-1]. Good. Ensure length exactly n; could if n=1 as above active low direct, but if FFT branch for n=1? active size maybe 1, max_active>=64, direct. fine.

`_lse_weights(b, mb, C)`:
```
def _lse_weights(b, mb, C=20.0):
    if mb <= 0:
       return np.full(b.size, 1.0/b.size)
    tau = max(10000.0, C / mb)
    tau = min(tau, 1e7)
    e = np.exp(tau * (b - mb))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       p = np.zeros_like(b)
       mask = b == mb
       if not mask.any(): mask[np.argmax(b)] = True
       p[mask] = 1.0 / mask.size? mask.sum()
       return p
    return e/s
```
`mask.sum()` returns int.

`run_adam` uses these.

Need local variable `conv` closure to use L:
```
L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
def conv(a):
   A=np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
   b=np.fft.irfft(A*A, n=L)
   return b[:2*n-1]
```
This allocates arrays each call. We can maybe preallocate A? Hard because a changes. Fine.

def _softmax(x):
   if x.size == 1: return np.ones(1)
   xm = x - x.max()
   e = np.exp(xm)
   s=e.sum()
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       out=np.full_like(x, 1.0/x.size); return out
   return e/s
```
If x max inf? x finite handled.

`gen_x`:
```
def _gen_x(m, rng, mode):
    x=np.zeros(m)
    if m<=0: return x
    if mode==0:
       x=np.zeros(m)
    elif mode==1:
       x=rng.normal(0.0,1.0,m)
    elif mode==2:
       spec=np.zeros(m//2+1, dtype=complex)
       freq_idxs=np.arange(spec.size)
       # keep low frequencies maybe
       mask = (freq_idxs <= max(1, m//8)) | (freq_idxs >= m - max(1,m//8))
       spec[mask] = rng.normal(size=mask.sum()) + 1j*rng.normal(size=mask.sum())
       x=np.fft.irfft(spec, n=m)
       x -= x.mean()
    elif mode==3:
       x=-6.0*np.ones(m)
       k=max(1, m//4)
       idx=rng.choice(m, size=k, replace=False)
       x[idx]=rng.normal(0.0,1.0)
    else:
       x=np.linspace(1.5,-1.5,m) + rng.normal(0.0,0.5,m)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): x=np.zeros(m)
    x -= x.mean()
    return x
```

Now, local search function:
```
def local_search(state, deadline_local):
    if time.monotonic() > deadline_local: return
    a = np.asarray(state['seq'], float)
    # normalize to sum1? update did. If a size 0 return
    best = _trim_normalize(a)
    best_score = state['score']
    # maybe exact re-evaluate best after trim
    sc = evaluate_sequence(best.tolist())
    if finite and sc < best_score: best_score=sc; best=...
    n=best.size
    if n==0: return
    max_iters = ...
    sigma=0.25
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time > deadline_local: break
       c = best.copy()
       move = rng.integers(0,6)
       if move==0:
          wdt=int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(n, n//50+2))))
          st=int(rng.integers(0, max(1,n-wdt+1)))
          c[st:st+wdt] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0, sigma,1)))
       elif move==1:
          idx=int(rng.integers(0,n)); c[idx] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0, 0.5,1)))
       elif move==2:
          i = int(np.argmax(np.random? use rng.random(n) * (best>0)?))? We'll use i = rng.integers(n); if best[i]<=0, pick random positive? 
          positives=np.nonzero(best>0)[0]; if len pos>0: i=rng.choice(positives); else i=0
          j=rng.integers(n); delta=best[i]*rng.uniform(0.05,0.5); c[i]-=delta; c[j]+=delta
       elif move==3:
          # add small bump at random
          wdt=...; st=...; c[st:st+wdt] += 0.05*best.max()*rng.uniform(-1,1)? nonneg clip later
       elif move==4:
          c *= np.exp(0.05*rng.normal(size=n))
       else:
          # random coordinate increases supports

       c = _trim_normalize(c)
       sc = evaluate_sequence(c.tolist())
       if finite and sc < best_score:
           best_score=sc; best=c; update state
```

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0: return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)

    s = float(a.sum())

       return np.array([1.0])

    return a

```python
import numpy as np
import time


def _softmax(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 1:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    x = x - float(x.max())
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.full_like(x, 1.0 / x.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return e / s


def _lse_weights(b, mb, C):
    b = np.asarray(b, dtype=np.float64)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if mb <= 0.0:
        return np.full(b.size, 1.0 / b.size, dtype=np.float64)
    tau = max(2000.0, C / max(mb, 1e-300))
    tau = min(tau, 1.0e7)
    e = np.exp(tau * (b - mb))
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        p = np.zeros_like(b, dtype=np.float64)
        mask = (b == mb)
        if not np.any(mask):
            mask[int(np.argmax(b))] = True
        cnt = int(np.count_nonzero(mask))
        if cnt > 0:
            p[mask] = 1.0 / cnt
        else:
            p[int(np.argmax(b))] = 1.0
        return p
    return e / s


def _gradient_from_p(p, a, n):
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 0:
        return g
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    pmax = float(p.max())
    if pmax <= 0.0:
        return g

    thresh = max(1e-15, pmax * 1e-7)
    active = np.nonzero(p > thresh)[0]

    max_active = max(64, min(5000, 2000000 // max(1, n)))
    if active.size <= max_active:
        for k in active:
            pk = float(p[k])
            if pk <= 0.0:
                continue
            fac = 2.0 * pk
            kk = int(k)
            if kk < n:
                g[:kk + 1] += fac * a[kk::-1]
            else:
                x0 = kk - n + 1
                if x0 < n:
                    g[x0:n] += fac * a[n - 1:x0 - 1:-1]
        return g

    m = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1 << max(1, (3 * n - 2).bit_length())
    p_rev = p[::-1]
    Fa = np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
    Fp = np.fft.rfft(p_rev, n=L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(Fa * Fp, n=L)
    stop = m - n - 1
    g[:] = 2.0 * c[m - 1:stop:-1]
    return g


def _trim_normalize(a, rel=1e-12):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

    if a.size > 1:
        maxv = float(a.max())
        if maxv > 0.0:
            tol = max(1e-18, maxv * rel)
            mask = a > tol
            if np.any(mask):
                first = int(np.argmax(mask))
                last = int(a.size - 1 - int(np.argmax(mask[::-1])))
                if first > 0 or last < a.size - 1:
                    a = a[first:last + 1]
            else:
                idx = int(np.argmax(a))
                a = a[idx:idx + 1]

    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def _gen_x(m, rng, mode):
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    if mode == 0:
        x = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    elif mode == 1:
        x = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, m).astype(np.float64)
    elif mode == 2:
        spec = np.zeros(m // 2 + 1, dtype=complex)
        low = max(1, m // 10)
        idx = np.arange(min(low + 1, spec.size))
        amp = 1.0 - idx / float(low + 1)
        spec[idx] = amp * (rng.normal(size=idx.size) + 1j * rng.normal(size=idx.size))
        x = np.fft.irfft(spec, n=m).astype(np.float64)
        x -= float(x.mean())
    elif mode == 3:
        x = -6.0 * np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
        k = max(1, m // 4)
        idx = rng.choice(m, size=k, replace=False)
        x[idx] = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, k)
    else:
        x = np.linspace(1.5, -1.5, m).astype(np.float64) + rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, m)

    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        x = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    x -= float(x.mean())
    return x


def _greedy_support(span, m, rng):
    span = int(span)
    m = int(m)
    if m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=int)
    if m >= span:
        return np.arange(span, dtype=int)
    if span <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=int)

    pos = np.array([0], dtype=int)
    present = np.zeros(span, dtype=bool)
    present[0] = True
    counts = np.zeros(2 * span - 1, dtype=np.int64)
    counts[0] += 1

    for _ in range(1, m):
        R = min(span, max(20, 2 * m))
        cands = rng.choice(span, size=R, replace=False)
        best_c = None
        best_score = None

        for c_raw in cands:
            c = int(c_raw)
            if present[c]:
                continue
            maxv = int(counts[2 * c]) + 1
            for p in pos:
                v = int(counts[c + int(p)]) + 2
                if v > maxv:
                    maxv = v
            score = float(maxv) + 1e-6 * float(rng.random())
            if best_score is None or score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_c = c

        if best_c is None:
            while True:
                c = int(rng.integers(0, span))
                if not present[c]:
                    break
            best_c = c

        old_pos = pos.copy()
        pos = np.sort(np.concatenate((pos, np.array([best_c], dtype=int))))
        counts[2 * best_c] += 1
        for p in old_pos:
            counts[best_c + int(p)] += 2
        present[best_c] = True

    return pos


def _gen_positions(span, m, rng, greedy=False):
    span = int(span)
    m = int(m)
    if span <= 0 or m <= 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=int)
    if m >= span:
        return np.arange(span, dtype=int)

    if greedy and m <= 220 and span <= 7000:
        try:
            p = _greedy_support(span, m, rng)
            if p.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
                p = np.asarray(p, dtype=int)
                p = p - p[0]
                return p
        except Exception:
            pass

    idx = rng.choice(span, size=m, replace=False)
    p = np.sort(idx).astype(int)
    p = p - p[0]
    return p


def _run_adam(n, pos, x0, steps, lr0, deadline, rng, stats, full):
    n = int(n)
    pos = np.asarray(pos, dtype=int)
    m = int(pos.size)
    if n <= 0 or m <= 0 or steps <= 0:
        return None, np.inf

    x = np.asarray(x0, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if x.size != m:
        x = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        x = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    x -= float(x.mean())

    L = 1 << max(1, (2 * n - 1).bit_length())

    def conv(a):
        A = np.fft.rfft(a, n=L)
        b = np.fft.irfft(A * A, n=L)
        return b[:2 * n - 1]

    if full:
        w = _softmax(x)
        a = w
    else:
        a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        w = _softmax(x)
        a[pos] = w

    b = conv(a)
    mb = float(b.max())
    score = 2.0 * float(n) * mb
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        score = np.inf

    best_x = x.copy()
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    last_improve = 0

    beta1 = 0.9
    beta2 = 0.99
    eps = 1e-8
    adam_m = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    adam_v = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    t = 0

    restarts = 0
    patience = max(100, steps // 10)
    noise_scale = 0.25

    for it in range(int(steps)):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

        tt = time.monotonic()
        frac = float(it) / float(max(1, steps))
        C = 5.0 + 25.0 * min(1.0, frac * 2.0)
        p = _lse_weights(b, mb, C)
        g = _gradient_from_p(p, a, n)

        gp = g if full else g[pos]
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(gp)):
            gp = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)

        dot_wg = float(np.dot(w, gp))
        grad = w * (gp - dot_wg)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
            grad = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)

        t += 1
        adam_m = beta1 * adam_m + (1.0 - beta1) * grad
        adam_v = beta2 * adam_v + (1.0 - beta2) * (grad * grad)
        bm = adam_m / (1.0 - beta1 ** t)
        vv = adam_v / (1.0 - beta2 ** t)

        lr = lr0 * (1.0 - 0.5 * min(1.0, frac))
        x = x - lr * bm / (np.sqrt(vv) + eps)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
            x[~np.isfinite(x)] = 0.0
        x -= float(x.mean())

        w = _softmax(x)
        if full:
            a = w
        else:
            a[pos] = w

        b = conv(a)
        mb = float(b.max())
        score = 2.0 * float(n) * mb

        if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score * (1.0 - 1e-12):
            best_score = score
            best_x = x.copy()
            best_a = a.copy()
            last_improve = it

        stats[0] = 0.9 * stats[0] + 0.1 * (time.monotonic() - tt)

        if it - last_improve > patience:
            if restarts >= 5:
                break
            x = best_x.copy()
            x += rng.normal(0.0, noise_scale, m).astype(np.float64)
            if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
                x = best_x.copy()
            x -= float(x.mean())
            adam_m.fill(0.0)
            adam_v.fill(0.0)
            t = 0
            restarts += 1
            patience = max(50, patience // 2)
            noise_scale *= 0.75

            w = _softmax(x)
            if full:
                a = w
            else:
                a[pos] = w
            b = conv(a)
            mb = float(b.max())
            score = 2.0 * float(n) * mb
            if not np.isfinite(score):
                break

    return best_a, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    base_budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))

    opt_margin = min(120.0, max(20.0, base_budget * 0.10))
    opt_deadline = t0 + max(5.0, base_budget - opt_margin)

    local_margin = min(30.0, max(10.0, base_budget * 0.05))
    local_deadline = t0 + max(10.0, base_budget - local_margin)

    final_deadline = t0 + max(0.1, base_budget - 5.0)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed if seed is not None else 42)
    stats = [0.005]

    state = {"score": np.inf, "seq": np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)}

    def update_state(arr):
        if time.monotonic() >= final_deadline - 34.0:
            return
        cand = _trim_normalize(arr)
        if cand.size == 0:
            cand = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
        try:
            sc = evaluate_sequence(cand.tolist())
        except Exception:
            return
        sc = float(sc)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < state["score"]:
            state["score"] = sc
            state["seq"] = cand.copy()

    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        parent = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)

    if parent.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(parent)):
        parent = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)

    parent = np.nan_to_num(parent, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    parent = np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1000.0)

    update_state(parent)

    s_parent = float(parent.sum())
    if np.isfinite(s_parent) and s_parent > 0.0:
        parent_norm = parent / s_parent
    else:
        parent_norm = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)

    update_state(parent_norm)

    parent_len = int(parent_norm.size)
    if parent_len <= 0:
        parent_len = 256

    try:
        parent_x = np.log(parent_norm + 1e-12)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(parent_x)):
            parent_x = np.zeros(parent_len, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        parent_x = np.zeros(parent_len, dtype=np.float64)
    parent_x -= float(np.mean(parent_x))

    dense_configs = [
        (64, 6, 800),
        (128, 6, 1000),
        (256, 6, 1200),
        (512, 4, 1400),
        (1024, 3, 1600),
        (2048, 2, 1800),
        (5582, 2, 2000),
    ]

    sparse_configs = [
        (256, 32, 4, 1000),
        (512, 48, 4, 1200),
        (1024, 72, 3, 1400),
        (2048, 104, 3, 1600),
        (4096, 144, 2, 1800),
        (5582, 122, 2, 2000),
        (3000, 96, 2, 1800),
        (6000, 176, 1, 2000),
    ]

    for n, support_count, steps in dense_configs:
        if time.monotonic() >= opt_deadline - 30.0:
            break
        for s in range(int(support_count)):
            if time.monotonic() >= opt_deadline - 30.0:
                break

            lr0 = 0.02 if n >= 2048 else 0.05
            if n == parent_len and s == 0:
                x0 = parent_x.copy()
                x0 += rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, n).astype(np.float64)
                if not np.all(np.isfinite(x0)):
                    x0 = _gen_x(n, rng, 1)
                x0 -= float(x0.mean())
            else:
                x0 = _gen_x(n, rng, s % 5)

            pos = np.arange(n, dtype=int)
            try:
                best_a, _ = _run_adam(
                    n, pos, x0, steps, lr0, opt_deadline, rng, stats, full=True
                )
            except Exception:
                best_a = None

            if best_a is not None:
                update_state(best_a)

    for span, m, support_count, steps in sparse_configs:
        if time.monotonic() >= opt_deadline - 30.0:
            break
        for s in range(int(support_count)):
            if time.monotonic() >= opt_deadline - 30.0:
                break

            greedy = (s == 0)
            pos = _gen_positions(span, m, rng, greedy=greedy)
            if pos.size == 0:
                continue
            n_act = int(pos[-1]) + 1
            if n_act <= 1:
                n_act = 1

            mode = (s + 1) % 5
            x0 = _gen_x(pos.size, rng, mode)
            lr0 = 0.025 if n_act >= 2048 else 0.05

            try:
                best_a, _ = _run_adam(
                    n_act, pos, x0, steps, lr0, opt_deadline, rng, stats, full=False
                )
            except Exception:
                best_a = None

            if best_a is not None:
                update_state(best_a)

    if time.monotonic() < local_deadline:
        try:
            best_arr = np.asarray(state["seq"], dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            best_arr = _trim_normalize(best_arr)
            if best_arr.size == 0:
                best_arr = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
            best_score = float(state["score"])
            if not np.isfinite(best_score):
                best_score = np.inf

            try:
                sc0 = evaluate_sequence(best_arr.tolist())
                if np.isfinite(sc0) and sc0 < best_score:
                    best_score = float(sc0)
            except Exception:
                pass

            n = int(best_arr.size)
            if n > 0:
                if n <= 256:
                    max_iters = 3000
                elif n <= 1024:
                    max_iters = 1200
                elif n <= 4096:
                    max_iters = 350
                else:
                    max_iters = 160

                for _ in range(max_iters):
                    if time.monotonic() >= local_deadline - 5.0:
                        break

                    c = best_arr.copy()
                    move = int(rng.integers(0, 6))
                    max_v = float(best_arr.max())

                    if move == 0:
                        width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(n, n // 50 + 2))))
                        width = max(1, min(width, n))
                        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
                        c[start:start + width] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, 1)))
                    elif move == 1:
                        idx = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                        c[idx] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5, 1)))
                    elif move == 2:
                        pos_idx = np.nonzero(best_arr > max_v * 1e-6)[0]
                        if pos_idx.size > 0:
                            i = int(pos_idx[int(rng.integers(0, pos_idx.size))])
                            j = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                            delta = best_arr[i] * float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.5))
                            c[i] -= delta
                            c[j] += delta
                    elif move == 3:
                        width = int(rng.integers(1, max(2, min(n, n // 100 + 2))))
                        width = max(1, min(width, n))
                        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
                        c[start:start + width] += max_v * float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.1))
                    elif move == 4:
                        c *= np.exp(0.05 * rng.normal(size=n))
                    else:
                        for _ in range(min(12, n)):
                            idx = int(rng.integers(0, n))
                            c[idx] += max_v * float(rng.uniform(0.0, 0.05))

                    c = _trim_normalize(c)
                    if c.size == 0:
                        continue
                    try:
                        sc = evaluate_sequence(c.tolist())
                    except Exception:
                        sc = np.inf
                    sc = float(sc)
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
                        best_score = sc
                        state["score"] = sc
                        state["seq"] = c.copy()
                        best_arr = c.copy()
                        max_v = float(best_arr.max())
        except Exception:
            pass

    final_arr = _trim_normalize(state["seq"])
    if final_arr.size == 0:
        final_arr = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    try:
        final_score = evaluate_sequence(final_arr.tolist())
    except Exception:
        final_score = np.inf

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        final_arr = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    final_arr = np.nan_to_num(final_arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    final_arr = np.clip(final_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if final_arr.size == 0:
        final_arr = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    return [float(v) for v in final_arr]
```
